# Cosmology 4 - The Fatal Error of Eratosthenes

**[00:00:00]** Here's a map of ancient Egypt. I've inserted two sticks or obelisks, one up here in Alexandria

**[00:00:15]** and one down here in Syene. Now, if at a certain moment each stick casts no shadow, no shadow

**[00:00:23]** at all, that's perfectly easy to understand, provided the earth is flat. If the shadow at Syene

**[00:00:30]** is at a certain length and the shadow at Alexandria is the same length, that also makes sense on a

**[00:00:36]** flat earth. But how could it be, Eratosthenes asked, that at the same instant there was no shadow at

**[00:00:44]** Syene and a very substantial shadow at Alexandria? The only answer,

**[00:00:53]** was that the surface of the earth is curved. Not only that, but the greater the curvature,

**[00:01:01]** the bigger the difference in the lengths of the shadows. The sun is so far away that its rays are

**[00:01:07]** parallel when they reach the earth. Sticks at different angles to the sun's rays will cast

**[00:01:12]** shadows of different lengths. For the observed difference in the shadow lengths, the distance

**[00:01:17]** between Alexandria and Syene had to be about seven degrees along the surface of the earth.

**[00:01:23]** By that I mean, if you imagine these sticks extending all the way down to the center of the earth,

**[00:01:30]** they would there intersect at an angle of about seven degrees. Well, seven degrees is something

**[00:01:36]** like a fiftieth of the full circumference of the earth, 360 degrees. Eratosthenes knew the distance

**[00:01:43]** between Alexandria and Syene. He knew it was 800 kilometers. Why? Because he hired a man to pace

**[00:01:51]** out the entire distance so that he could pass the entire distance. He knew that the distance between

**[00:01:53]** the earth and the sun was about a hundred kilometers. He knew that the distance between Alexandria and

**[01:26:58]** Then imagine that you have to do the same.

**[01:27:00]** But they don't tell you that up there

**[01:27:02]** there is a lens that refracts the light.

**[01:27:04]** When the refraction occurs,

**[01:27:06]** it becomes impossible to triangulate the light source,

**[01:27:08]** especially if you don't know

**[01:27:10]** which lens was used.

**[01:27:12]** Do you see how this works?

**[01:27:14]** Here is the light from the light source

**[01:27:16]** emanating in different places,

**[01:27:18]** but it is hitting some sort of refractor.

**[01:27:20]** Now who knows what this is?

**[01:27:22]** You can speculate.

**[01:27:24]** We will talk about that throughout this series.

**[01:27:26]** But there is a firmament of something,

**[01:27:28]** whether it is immaterial or atmospheric

**[01:27:30]** or it is ether or it is maybe the water.

**[01:27:32]** Who knows what it is?

**[01:27:34]** But there is something refracting the light.

**[01:27:36]** Those light rays,

**[01:27:38]** once they hit this medium,

**[01:27:40]** they disperse at different angles.

**[01:27:42]** So for example,

**[01:27:44]** if you see this in this drawing,

**[01:27:46]** this light ray,

**[01:27:48]** if you were here on this part of the table

**[01:27:50]** trying to use the angle

**[01:27:52]** to triangulate the position of the sun,

**[01:27:54]** you are going to get over here.

**[01:27:56]** In fact, you can see this little highlighter

**[01:27:58]** on my mouse is actually about the same size as the picture.

**[01:28:00]** As the sun in this picture.

**[01:28:02]** Do you see the problem?

**[01:28:04]** So if you use this line,

**[01:28:06]** if you draw it all the way up,

**[01:28:08]** it is right about here.

**[01:28:10]** This is where the apparent sun would be.

**[01:28:12]** But the actual sun is here.

**[01:28:14]** Do you see the problem?

**[01:28:16]** So according to the angle that you see here

**[01:28:18]** through this lens,

**[01:28:20]** your sun is over here,

**[01:28:22]** but it is actually over here.

**[01:28:24]** Then this person, same thing.

**[01:28:26]** If this one tries to draw it,

**[01:28:28]** it is going to be over here.
